As a result of the work undertaken in both the Permanent Sub-Committee on amendments to criminal law as well as the main committee on 22 July 2021, I have been able to present the final legislative product and therefore propose the adoption of the bill as proposed by the committee. Ladies and gentlemen! Despite a number of corrections, the main core of the original project was retouched, the bone of the applicants' assumptions was maintained. The project provides for increased consequences and additional ailments for the perpetrators of corruption acts. Persons convicted of a corruption offence, enumerated in the assumptions, may be deprived of public rights as well as criminal measures against them in the form of a ban on the performance of functions or occupations. In the event of a criminal offence, such a prohibition shall be imposed for life. Ladies and gentlemen, this will result in both preventive and retributive functions of criminal law. Ladies and gentlemen, in the original assumption, these records were slightly toothless, namely there was no criminalisation of the behaviour of breaking this ban, namely the ban on the performance of functions or professions. As a result of the changes there will be penalization, the article will be modified. 244 criminal code. Ladies and gentlemen! There will also be a ban on Members and Senators, as well as parliamentarians. You might say it's gonna be a In addition, new rules will be introduced to ensure greater transparency and transparency in public life. Political parties will have to present the sources of funding, as well as the expenditure they make for their actions. It should be emphasized that these most generous party benefactors will also have to be shown, payments here will be at the level of 10,000. zł. In addition, it is envisaged that the State Election Commission shall be equipped with tools to punish operators in a party where this notification obligation will not be carried out. Ladies and gentlemen! In conclusion, this bill is an opportunity to remedy the situation in an area where legislative interference will be carried out. It should be stressed that this will further increase the anti-corruption security and increase the transparency of public life. I would like to finish by thanking Mr Bartłomiej Wróblewski, chairman of the criminal committee team, who has done very, very much work at this first stage - we as the main committee have finished - as well as Mr Jarosław Sachajka, the representative of the applicants, for good cooperation. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-15-sejm-37-1.u28	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Circle15 - Direct Democracy, I would like to thank all the members of the committee who have engaged in the work on this project, with its president, Piotr Sak, at the head. Mediaally, the project is called an anti-corruption act. I would also like to thank the Ministry of Justice with the present Minister Marcin Warchoł for his valuable remarks. Corruption is usually seen as political action. Unfortunately, it is undervalued by its disastrous impact on economic and social life. Knowing the disastrous impact of corruption, we propose harsh solutions that will be preventive and will effectively protect our homeland from the devastating phenomenon of corruption. The project assumes some fundamental changes. Firstly, the catalogue of crimes has been extended, which will result in very severe sanctions, because we are amending not only the Penal Code, but also the Sports and Drug Refund Act. The harsh sanctions I was just talking about are mandatory job losses, and twice for corruption - a life ban on working in public institutions. Public bodies should have particular trust. Secondly, there has been a ban on those convicted of public procurement, so we cut them off from public money. Thirdly, we allow the court to deprive the convicted of corruption of public rights. In this way, we exclude the possibility of applying for the mandate of the mayor, the President, the Member and the Senator. Fourthly, the mayors, mayors, presidents and Members and Senators will not be able to be employed in companies belonging to the State Treasury or local government units. Fifthly, we introduce greater transparency in the functioning of political parties through the obligation to keep and update a register of payments that will be placed in the Public Information Bulletin. Sixthly, political parties will be required to keep and keep up to date a register of contracts concluded and amended, which will also be included in the Public Information Bulletin. Seventhly, transparency is introduced in the public finances of local governments through the obligation to publish a register of contracts together with their changes on the pages of the Public Information Bulletin. These solutions prevent such harmful corruption and are only some of the proposals contained in this law. Circle of Poselskie Kukiz.15 - Direct Democracy supports the solutions developed. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-15-sejm-37-1.u78	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present a report to the Committee on Digitization, Innovation and Modern Technologies on the Government's draft law on amending the Act on Personal Evidence, the Act on Records, the Law on Civil Status Acts and some other laws, prints no. 1399 and 1480. Draft No 1399 was the subject of the committee's work on 10 August 2021 when the first reading of the draft law took place. The project introduces amendments mainly in three laws governing so-called. civil affairs where the introduction of the PESEL register, the Register of Personal Evidence and the Register of Civil State respectively has been specified. The project assumes the adoption of legal bases... [[Who's in charge?]] [[Voice from the hall: No marshal?]] There's no marshal? [[Room voice: I'm declaring a recess.]] Oh, good, you're here. The project provides for the adoption of legal bases for the use of electronic services available after the authentication of the citizen. These services will help speed up the handling of cases, reduce the burden on officials, speed up the delivery of services and reduce the number of documents supplied and collected in paper form. They will allow citizens to save money on taxes. The services will be implemented on the gov site. pl, which is a fundamental source of knowledge for citizens in the field of specific official matters, using a trusted profile or a personal profile. The proposed amendments include a package of electronic services in the area of civil affairs, the common denominator of which is the transactionation and automation of processes and services for citizens using the registers introduced by the Minister for Digitisation and the use of an electronic seal. From these registers, citizens will be able to download documents containing information on the entities to which their personal data has been made available. Within the scope of the Personal Evidence Act, applicants will be provided with the possibility to make their personal data available from the Register of Personal Evidence in a limited mode of data transmission and to submit electronic documents confirming the verification of statutory conditions for granting access. From the scope of the People's Register Act, the person concerned, using transactional services, will be able to report his/her departure from the country with the intention of permanent residence and return, check-in at the premises and check-out from the premises owned or co-owned by him/her, provided that he/she is listed in the perpetual books or if the number of the book is indicated. The electronic notification of the birth of the child shall ensure that a copy of the summary birth certificate, the certificate of registration of the child and the notification of the designation No PESEL are available. The proposed amendments will allow access to the PESEL register for personal data of that person and the collection of a certificate containing a complete or partial copy of the data. Amendments to the Act amending the Act on Personal Evidence, the Act on Civil Records, the Act - The Law on Civil Status Records and some other laws will allow the person whose data are processed in the Register of Civil Status, his/her spouse, children and parents to collect a certificate of data from the Register of Civil Status using electronic services and to collect a certificate of civil status. Certificates taken from the register in electronic form shall be stamped by an electronic stamp of the Minister responsible for computerization and verified by means of a qualified certificate. All of these services will be available to minors by a parent who has been made available from the PESEL register for his child's personal data and under whose parental authority the child remains. High Seym! The Committee on Digitisation, Innovation and Modern Technologies, following its first reading and the examination of the project, together with 13 legislative amendments, proposes that the proposed bill be adopted by the High Sejm. As they were purely technical, the amendments were adopted by block and the whole draft committee unanimously supported. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The aim of this bill is to enable citizens to handle basic official matters in a remote form by means of simple, intuitive and dedicated electronic services. As the government assures, it will be quicker and more convenient, as well as easier, for those who are just taking their first steps on the Internet. It is also important in the context of the processed law of experience which the state administration acquires during the COVID-19 pandemic, when the scale of remotely issued documents has significantly increased. Point 1 of the draft law is to be launched on 3 March 2022. Thus, the proposed amendment aims to correlate the date of entry into force of the law with the date on which the service will actually be launched. This provision does not oblige the courts to use this procedure from the date of entry into force of the rules, but only gives them the opportunity to do so. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-15-sejm-37-1.u110	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of a group of Members of the applicants a draft law amending the excise duty Act, print No 1510. This is a draft law that makes it very easy for businesses in the tax warehouse to operate, removes excise barriers and is as desirable and expected as possible by those companies who, in the course of activities related to tax warehouses, carry out daily activities related to excise products. But ad rem. In accordance with the provisions of the Excise Tax Act, the inclusion of excise goods in the tax warehouse allows the introduction of a suspension of excise duty. One of the reasons for ending the suspension of excise duty is the withdrawal of the tax warehouse permit. The purpose of the proposed law is to allow for the continuation of the activities carried out in the tax warehouse in the event that the existing tax warehouse operator resigns from those activities without having to complete the suspension procedure for excise goods in that tax warehouse. Such a situation may arise in the event of the disposal of ownership of the property in which the tax warehouse is situated to the purchaser who will maintain the tax warehouse in the same place. The current rules prevent the continuation of activities in the form of a tax warehouse by another entity which is not the successor to the tax warehouse operator. The whole production process must be discontinued in the production-type tax warehouses, in view of the need to terminate their activities and to settle excise duties as a result of the suspension of excise duty. An entity wishing to take over this tax warehouse with excise goods under suspension of excise duty is currently not in a position to do so. The exception to this rule applies only to legal successors, i.e. entities which enter into the rights and obligations of the existing taxable person in the event of merger, acquisition, conversion or division. In other cases, the change in the tax warehouse operator requires the withdrawal of the excise licence to the existing tax warehouse operator and the issue of a new excise permit to the new entity. The withdrawal of a tax warehouse permit shall result in the termination of the excise duty suspension procedure for all excise goods in the tax warehouse on the date of the withdrawal of the licence, which means the payment of excise duty on those products to such an economic operator. The current regulations can therefore constitute a barrier to economic activity and the aim of applicants is to remove these barriers. The proposed amendment will allow the tax warehouse to be taken over in cases other than tax succession, without the need to abolish the activities carried out in the tax warehouse and to pay excise duties on excise goods in that warehouse. If we are talking about these proposed solutions, they remove the difficulties in taking over the tax warehouse, eliminating a number of very labour-intensive and cost-intensive activities on the part of companies expanding their tax warehouses or continuing their activities in place of the predecessor in the tax composition regime in a continuous, harmonious manner. We can describe it as such a smooth acquisition and continuation of activity. The social, economic, financial and legal impact of the proposed changes will be positive both from the point of view of taxpayers, facilitating their functioning and the State Treasury powered by public tributes resulting from the activities of prosperous enterprises. The preparation of these new regulations, both on the tax side of the tax administration and on the side of entrepreneurs, does not require additional, unnecessary time and longer vacathio legis, and therefore it seems justified to enter into force of the proposed law on the day following the date of publication. The entry into force of the proposed law will not, as I would like to stress, increase in expenditure on the part of the state budget or budgets of local government units. The proposed regulation should potentially have a positive impact on the activities of those micro-entrepreneurs, small and medium-sized enterprises that run or intend to maintain a tax warehouse. For other entrepreneurs belonging to these groups, this regulation presented in the draft law will be neutral. I therefore propose and ask the High House to adopt the proposed bill, which, once again, I will stress, will be very positive for those entrepreneurs who run tax warehouses today. Thank you very much, Marshal.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-15-sejm-37-1.u115	Thank you very much. Mr Wiesław Szczepański, parliamentary club of the Left. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-15-sejm-37-1.u116	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the parliamentary club of the Left, I have the honour to present to the High House our position on the parliamentary draft law amending the excise duty Act in print 1510. The proposal we are discussing today amends the excise duty Act of 6 December 2008. A regulation is adopted to allow for the continuation of the activities carried out in the tax warehouse in the event of a withdrawal from the activities of the former entity. According to the applicants, it will not be necessary to complete the suspension procedure for excise goods in that warehouse. It follows from the justification of the Act that such a situation may take place in the case of the sale of the property in which the tax warehouse is situated to the purchaser who will be the owner of the property at that point. And as my previous speaker said, perhaps today the submission of this bill is part of the solution to the problem of Lotus and Orlen and the costs that would have to be paid at the time of the sale of this part of the property and the fuel there, i.e. the payment of excise duties. However, I will discuss this from the point of view of small entrepreneurs. The proposed solution has long been expected by many small operators carrying out such activities. They pointed out that there was no possibility for the successor to a legal entity that had not previously operated tax warehouses through the purchase of real estate. At present, the acquirer of the existing tax warehouse, together with excise goods under suspension of excise duty, is not able to do so, except, of course, legal successors. What are tax warehouses and how is the tax warehouse allowed? The answer is this. You produce, store excise goods, e.g. alcoholic beverages, gas or cigarettes, and you don't want to pay excise duty. You import products from abroad without excise duty, someone has ordered you to perform this service activity. In order to do this, you need to be allowed to operate a tax warehouse business. Therefore, the tax warehouse is the place where excise goods can be produced, processed and stored without payment of excise duty until the introduction of the products concerned from the excise duty suspension system. As a result, the movement of excise goods using the tax warehouse results in the deferral of the payment of excise duty on the said products and transfers that obligation for the period during which the products leave the warehouse for their release into free circulation. In order to be authorised to operate a tax warehouse, the applicant must comply with a number of requirements: apply for a storage permit, provide documents on the legal title of the property in which the business will be carried out, submit a storage plan, lodge an excise security. This is a long-term process and I understand today the entrepreneurs who, by taking over such property, want this procedure to be shortened and the other party did not have to pay such a large amount if there were, say, materials there as a result of production or if they are in storage. The proposed legislation will allow, in all cases where there is no universal tax succession, for another entity to take over the tax warehouse without having to liquidate the activities carried out in the storage and payment of excise duties on excise goods in that warehouse. Sometimes it's about a very large amount of money. Therefore, this solution will ensure continuity of the production composition, including the application of excise duty in the case of the tax composition of the production type, and perhaps sometimes the retention of the people who worked there and would have to be released. Businesses are particularly concerned with the difficulties of mainly the activities of the tax warehouse of the production type in which, in connection with the cessation of business and the clearance of excise duties as a result of the suspension of excise duty, traders must stop the entire production process. At present, the rules do not in principle permit the continuation of activities in tax warehouse by an entity other than the existing tax warehouse operator. And this bill, gentlemen, that the Members who have submitted this proposal, are changing that. According to today's law, on the date of withdrawal of the tax warehouse permit, the excise duty procedure is terminated. This means that an entrepreneur operating there is forced to pay sometimes very large amounts. It's about to change. My club supports the proposed amendment and requests that it be transferred to the competent Sejm Committee for further proceedings. [[Applause]] [[Chairman of the proceedings includes Deputy Marshal Małgorzata Kidawa-Błońska Sejm]]

We proceed to the examination of item 6 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law on the support of local government units in connection with the Polish Load Program (print No. 1531). I ask the Secretary of State at Sebastian Skuza's Ministry of Finance to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-15-sejm-37-1.u192	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a bill on the support of local government units in connection with the Polish Ład programme. The financing of local government is an extremely important issue both from the point of view of local government units themselves and of the entire public finance system. The law on the income of local government units is already several years old and requires discussion. Such dialogue shall be conducted on a committee of common government and local government. These talks may not be easy, but they are constructive and we have come to the conclusion that some changes are needed. Of course, not without significance for the finances of local government units are solutions proposed in the tax system under the Polish Load program. It is known that the historical tax reduction proposed in the Polish Łódź will have an impact on the finances of local government units. Therefore, we are presenting a draft law, where the starting point is that the financial system of the local government ensures greater stability and predictability in terms of revenue of local government units. What solutions have been prepared? You could say that we divide them into two groups. The first group is current, at hoc, and the second group is targeted. As far as current actions are concerned, I would like to mention first of all financial support for the implementation of the tasks of local self-government units with the amount of PLN 8 billion in 2021. The proposed regulations form the legal basis for the transfer of additional resources from the state budget to local government units in 2021 as a complement to the general subsidy. It is proposed that the allocation algorithm should be based on the participation of local government units in PIT planned for 2022, which is the consequence of the fact that due to the solutions of the Polish Government, the greatest financial consequences for the public finance sector, including for local government units, are generated in the case of PIT revenues. This division would also take into account the level of wealth of local self-government units. In the case of ad hoc solutions, it is also proposed to make the management of the budgets of local government units more flexible by modifying fiscal rules. Currently there are solutions to protect local government units from over-indebtedness. This is the principle of balancing the current budget and limiting the possibility of commitments. This is based on the so-called. the individual debt ratio of each local government unit. The draft law proposes the temporary application of preferences on the principle of balancing part of the current budget of local government units, i.e. in the medium term of four years in certain years the current budget of the local government unit could be unsustainable, but it would have to be balanced throughout the four-year period. In 2022, local government units would also be able to balance part of the current budget with free credits, loans from previous years. What else about changes in current actions? One further change in the individual limit on repayment of liabilities would be the proposal to change the option for local government units to choose the period on which the average operating surplus is calculated. This would be the choice between the three-year arithmetic average and the target seven-year arithmetic average. Local government units would have a temporary choice here. What is proposed in the target solutions? The first thing is: at the request of local government units themselves, the threshold for the European Union's contribution to the financing of programmes has been reduced from the debt limit. This means that if a programme is currently financed by more than 60% of EU funds, it is not included in the individual debt ratio. At the request of local government units themselves, this percentage was reduced to 50%. In turn, what target changes does the bill propose? First of all, the stabilisation and predictability of income. From next year, the project foresees that local government units would receive PIT and CIT shares in 1/12 of the planned appropriations for the financial year in question, i.e. as in the case of subsidies, they would have a regular income of a known amount of - 1/12. The next solution - I think - important for local government units is the issue of basing the correction and compensation mechanism on current PIT and CIT receipts. There is a two-year shift between the year on which the payments are calculated and the year on which the payments are made in the current correction and equalisation scheme. It's about the Janosik issue, for example. it would be paid in year n during the deconstructive period, and the calculation would be from the period n-2, i.e. the economic period. The project also provides for a pro-investment solution by introducing a new part of the general subsidy, so-called. the developmental part. And here I must stress that the development part will not lose the quality of the subsidy and will be able to be used for all tasks of local government units and will be treated in the budgets of local government units as current income, i.e. it will also be possible to finance current expenditure. It has this name: developmental, because there are some incentive mechanisms in place, so that local government units will invest as much as possible of these funds. This development part of the subsidy would consist of the basic amount, the activation premium and the investment premium. The basic amount would be allocated to each local government unit and would be divided in proportion to the number of inhabitants of the local government unit concerned, with the definition of minimum and maximum amounts. An activation premium would be granted to a local government unit which, per capita, would have such investment expenditure above average. The investment premiere would in turn be granted to local government units in which the ratio of property expenditure per capita will simply increase in proportion to the property expenditure of the local government units, respectively, i.e. the percentage, the percentage increase will be higher than the average. This part of the promotional subsidy is linked to a mechanism that protects local government units against the decline of CIT and PIT income. Namely, the draft law provides for a mechanism that, in the financial year in question, starting from 2023, income from participation in CIT and PIT will not be lower than the reference amount which local government units themselves have forecast in the multiannual financial forecast for 2022, even before the effects of the Polish Government, i.e. it can be said that on the old rules. This amount would be valued at nominal GDP growth, i.e. taking into account economic growth and inflation. If in 2023 it turned out that e.g. because of the effects of the losses of the Polish Deal there is a gap, this investment subsidy, the promotional subsidy must fill this gap. So it's like this. The gap of PLN 6 billion, the promotional subsidy must be PLN 6 billion. If this gap were not to exist, then the government is obliged by law to secure the amount of PLN 3 billion of investment subsidies also valued by nominal GDP growth. Thus, local government units would gain a certain amount from participation in CIT and PIT. There were voices here that it would be better for local government units to increase their share of income taxes, but I would point out that this would not work, for example. in small and medium-sized municipalities. As I assume that everyone or almost everyone supports the tax wedge, i.e. an increase in the tax-free amount and then in municipalities where there are a lot of low-income pensioners, where people work on minimum wages or a little higher, an increase in the percentage share would have done nothing. The arithmetic is simple because 100% from 0 or little more than 0 gives a small amount. Finally, I would also like to refer to the speech of Minister Barbara Socha and to make it clear that, as regards the issue of current action grants, namely here under Title 500+, these measures are not included in the formula for the individual debt ratio in Article. 242. They're neutral. The current budget is also neutral because revenue equals expenditure. Ladies and gentlemen! Thank you so much for your attention. I'm at your disposal. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-15-sejm-37-1.u195	Thank you very much. Please, Mr Jacek Protas, the Civic Coalition.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-15-sejm-37-1.u196	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Coalition presents our position on the draft law on the support of local government units in connection with the Polish Load programme. In the beginning, I would like to agree with the statement in the explanatory memorandum of the project that the 2003 Act on the Income of Local Government Units requires profound changes, but in fact it needs to be rewritten, because it does not take into account the new situation and new tasks that have been entrusted to local governments for 17 years. However, Minister, the ministry's work over the past six years has not ended with the expected project, and the local government is expecting it, and I think we all are. In the meantime, the government has proposed, and most of the Sejm has adopted, a number of legislative changes that destabilise the finances of the local government. The laws of August 2019 and November 2020 concerning tax solutions, mainly reductions in PIT and CIT, have reduced the revenue of local government units in 2019 and 2020 by a total amount of PLN 12 399 million. I don't think you're jealous of that, sir. In addition, local authorities were burdened with, and are, more spending on the maintenance of education. In 2014, the education grant covered 66% expenditure on the maintenance of schools and establishments, in 2019 only 60%. These income losses conflict with the Article. 167 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland principle of adequacy of financial supply to the scope of tasks. However, according to government data, tax changes in the Polish Łódź are expected to result in a decrease of revenue of local government units by a further 12 billion PLN per year. On the other hand, the proposals to compensate for these losses contained in the bill that we are discussing today are far from sufficient. First, they do not take into account the current decline in income of the JST in the years 2019 to 2021. The relevant projects concerning these compensations were submitted by us in the Sejm and adopted by the Senate. Unfortunately, they're in your Marshal's freezer. Secondly, the proposals presented in the project do not increase the participation of local authorities in PIT and CIT taxes, as mentioned by the Minister, despite the estimated reduction of these revenues as a result of the introduction of the Polish Order. Thirdly, they complicate the already simple system of financing tasks carried out by local governments. New algorithms are being introduced, new types of subsidies are being introduced. On the other hand, local governments expect the stabilisation of their own income. For parliamentarians of the Civic Coalition, but also for local governments, the bill in this form is unacceptable. Nor has it been agreed by the Joint Committee on Government and Territorial Self-Government, sir. Why, if you want to do so well for the local governments? Its adoption would result in a further drastic reduction in the revenue of local authorities and, consequently, in the inhibition of investment and deterioration of the quality of public services. Schools, kindergartens, daycare, social services, health care, public transport, the quality of roads and pavements - this is what local authorities lay down. Therefore, at tomorrow's meeting of the Joint Committees, the Civic Coalition will, after consultation with representatives of local governments, make amends to compensate local authorities for their losses so far and ensure that their level of income is appropriate to their tasks by means of a percentage increase in PIT and CIT taxes. Assuming the good will of the government and of the parliament, we hope to support our substantive proposals. The level and stability of the funding of local authorities depends directly on the quality of life of Poles and Poles, availability to health care, education, culture, sport and recreation (The Bell), public transport, air quality, state of the roads, pavements and public facilities. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-15-sejm-37-1.u200	Mrs. Marshal! Gentlemen of Ministers! High Izbo! Dear Marshal! Local self-government is a great achievement of the democratic transformation of Poland, awakening for three decades a sense of trust in Poles and highlighting the fundamental principle and role of local governments in the territorial system of the country. The political will to strengthen the communities of local municipalities, powiats, cities and voivodships was a source of strength for the whole state and we would all like to keep it that way. Po-covid's daily reality shows the enormous uncertainty faced by local governments. An example of this state of affairs and timeless decisions about changes in the administrative boundaries of cities and municipalities can be found in the municipality of Łobień Wielkie, whose representatives at the meeting of the local government committee yesterday stressed and showed the financial problems that the municipality is facing itself, but these financial problems in the near future may also affect other local authorities. This uncertainty is also aggravated by the lack of State compensation for losses in the income of current self-government units, the absence of local authorities' participation in VAT, the absence of a VAT exemption system, the absence of a participation in PIT. In recent years, it has also become a common principle that local government units are burdened by new sentences by the government, disregarding the financial compensation mechanism. [[What?]] Self-governments aren't afraid. They are very close to people, they know their needs, they want to make investments, but they fear that even the investment subsidy set out in the draft law, which will be a property income, will not complement the loss of shares in PIT receipts, which are a reduction in current income. According to the calculations of the Ministry of Finance, the central budget will gain about PLN 27 billion in 10 years, while local government will lose around PLN 1/4 of temporary income from participation in PIT tax - that is about PLN 150 billion. This can cause 1/3 of the local government units to lose liquidity immediately, their net operating surplus will fall below zero. A large number of other local authorities will find it difficult to finance current expenditure related to e.g. with education, social assistance, road maintenance. A few will only maintain the possibility of development. I would like to mention that the net operating surplus of all local authorities, which amounted to PLN 12 billion in 2020, will fall to around PLN 1.2 billion. In view of the above, it is appropriate to introduce a mechanism to compensate for losses in current income by e.g. exemption from VAT on goods and services purchased for the implementation by local authorities of investment projects. That's for example. it has been strongly defined by the Silesian Association of Municipalities and Districts, which is the largest regional self-government corporation in Poland. Such actions would certainly stimulate investment in local government activity and directly affect the maintenance or improvement of the quality of life of residents. In addition, this would be part of the principle of subsidiarity, decentralisation and autonomy of local and regional self-government units in determining the performance of their own tasks and shaping local and regional development. High Izbo! After a detailed analysis of the draft, which will take place tomorrow at the meeting of the Joint Committees, we will present the relevant amendments to this draft law and define our position on this matter. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Finally, June 1939 - The United States disagrees with the arrival of a St. Louis liner in Florida. There are 900 Jewish refugees from Germany on board. The ship turns around, the vast majority of passengers soon die in the gas chambers. Many times in our history Poles fleeing war and persecution have found refuge in other countries. Many times it was Poland that became a safe haven for refugees. That's our human destiny. The right to asylum is not included in the Geneva Convention, in European law, in the Polish Constitution by chance. It is the fundamental right of every human being, just as the basic need of every human being is security and seeking refuge for himself and his loved ones, often from war, from torture, from persecution. We are talking about completely basic issues, deeply rooted in European culture, in Christian tradition, in the Polish tradition of hospitality. And yet the ruling right wants to throw it all in the trash. He wants to give up respect for the fundamental human right to refuge and does so on the occasion of the first better provocation of the neighbouring regime, because that is de facto the law. It is an abandonment of the right to asylum, an attempt to legalize what is already happening on our border, an attempt to legalize the removal of people into the forest, to force them on the Belarusian side, an attempt to legalize practices that may be suited to the Belarusian dictatorship, but no longer democratic Poland. It is the responsibility of Poland to verify asylum applications also for those who cross the green border. It is in the light of international law, in the light of European law, that leaves no doubt in the light of the Polish Constitution. We just can't throw these people out of the country without seeing if they're threatened by death, torture, or persecution. We can't make decisions like this without being able to cancel. Let's really respect our intelligence. The investigation of your rights before a Polish court from Afghanistan, Somalia or Syria is pure fiction, and you know it. This bill you want to throw into the basket something extremely valuable, something that has accompanied us for a long time, openness to loved ones in need, a piece of ordinary humanity. I wanted to show you something. This is Mariam, she's 16. She recently went to school in Kabul. On 14 August 2021 the 80th anniversary of the martyred death of Maximilian Maria Kolbe, Polish Franciscan, missionary and Catholic Church is celebrated. Maximilian Maria Kolbe was born in Zduńska Wola. He came from a family that worked in weaving, and his ancestors were emigrants from the Czech Republic. In the church in Pabianice he found a calling to serve God and man. After his doctorate in Rome, he was able to find a modus vivendi with the Japanese authorities to carry out pastoral and missionary activities in Nagasaki with great success. He was the founder of the Immaculate Knights movement, the monthly Knight of Immaculate Knights and the Immaculate Radio. He was ahead of his time, appreciating the growing role of the media in shaping principles of social life based on Christian values. Since the first days of World War II, this holy Catholic Church, in accordance with the Franciscan spirit, has opened the gates of the monastery in Niepokalanów for refugees, sick and hungry, both for Christians and Jews. He sacrificed his life in the Nazi German concentration and extermination camp of Auschwitz by saving the family's father - Franciszek Gajowniczka. On the 80th anniversary of the death of St.Maximilian Maria Kolbe Sejm of the Republic of Poland, appreciating his merits for the Polish people and witnessing the love of his neighbor, he pays tribute to the martyr and honors his memory. The Commission proposes that this resolution be adopted. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-15-sejm-37-1.u367	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! At the age of 16, he joined the Franciscan Order in Lviv and was named Maximilian. Then he went to Rome, where he obtained PhDs in philosophy, theology. In 1917 he founded the Immaculate Knights' Association, and a year later he accepted priestly ordination. After his return to Poland in 1919, he founded a monastery in Niepokalanów near Warsaw and began to publish a letter "Knight of the Immaculate One." Successes achieved in Poland did not satisfy the monk. In order to win the whole world for the Immaculate, he left in 1930 for a mission to China and then to Japan. Even though some of his brothers thought it was crazy to go to the Far East, Father Kolbe created another Immaculate in Nagasaki. He published the Japanese equivalent of the Immaculate Knight and founded a novitiate and seminary. In 1936, the monk returned to Poland, where later World War II found him. He was arrested by the Gestapo, interrogated on Pawiak. On 28 May 1941, Father Maximilian Kolbe went to the Auschwitz-Birkenau extermination camp. He was assigned number 16,670. Father Kolbe was full of immense peace, comforting and comforting the convicts at night. He wanted to inspire the will to live and survive, and he mentioned one of the survivors. He said to his companions, "Trust the Immaculate One, you young people will live, I will not survive the camp, but you will be saved." In July 1941, after the escape of one of the prisoners, the camp commander, lagerführer Karl Fritzsch, sentenced 10 people to starvation. Come on, let's go. Maximilian was not in this group, he voluntarily switched and offered his life for the unknown Francis the Gay Man. At that time he justified his decision: I am almost 50 years old, I have lived my life, and he has a life ahead of him, he has a wife and children. Surprised German, who asked why he wanted to die for a stranger, answered: "I want to add courage to life to others." Franciscan died on 14 August 1941 with a phenol injection, the last of the prisoners locked up in a hunger bunker in the basement of block 11, so-called. Death block. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-15-sejm-37-1.u387	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! I would like to present from this place one of the many stories of my region from the period of the German occupation, a story that will forever be remembered by residents, families and the entire local community, and its commemoration is also my duty. July 24 is a special date for all residents of the municipality of Sulkowice. In 1943 there was a bloody and cruel pacification in this village, which resulted in the so-called. 26 people were murdered in the plebanian barn - the inhabitants of Sulkowice and Harbutowice. The pacification action was carefully planned and prepared by the German occupier. According to witnesses, there were 2 to 3 thousand in the area of Sułkowice. soldiers. A large number of soldiers could testify that the Germans were afraid of the intervention of guerrilla troops, which were located in nearby forests. It all started at night, at the turn of 23 and 24 July 1943. Just before the hour. The first 24 cars crossed the Sulkowice border. The pacification troops came to Sułkowice at the same time from all sides. About an hour. 4:00 a.m., people began to be expelled from their homes. After finding people from so-called. A blacklist was made of their arrests. At the market, the Nazis started preparing for interrogation. From all over the village groups were led to the center, to the cattle market. A crowd of people surrounded by a sealed cordon of armed gendarmes was moving across the road. All this time, people were pushed, beaten, and ridiculed. They saw their neighbors being pulled out of their homes and ready to march. The soldiers fueled the fear of the inhabitants, telling them with a smile that they would die, and the whole village would be burned down if they found weapons in one house or bypass. Spent on the cattle market, men were told to lay their heads to the ground with their hands outstretched. Obedience was threatened by physical punishment and even death by execution. The whole cattle square was made up of people. There was about $3,000 in there. persons. The sun was burning mercilessly, pregnant women fainted from exhaustion. There was no mercy for anyone. People on the so-called. The black leaf of the Gestapo led under the pleban barn. At the command of the Gestapos, they stood up and entered one by one in front of a pretend-court, which was located in the middle of a barn. That's where the beastly interrogations took place. People were beaten and tortured. Later, executions of dying and dead persons took place behind the pleban barn. The victims were executed by gunshot wound to the back of the head. Those who survived the interrogation were to be the first to be deported to forced labor. Okay, okay, okay, okay, okay, okay, okay, okay, okay, okay, okay, okay, okay, okay, okay, okay, okay, okay. 16 residents were released to their homes. The Gestapos banned the funeral of the murdered. Only after the war, in 1945. Their bodies were exhumed and they were solemnly buried in the parish cemetery. A social committee was set up in 1971. Its purpose was to erect a monument in the place of the tragedy commemorating the victims of those events, as well as all the people of Sulkowice who were killed by the Gestapo, died in Nazi camps or during the fight against the occupier. The monument was unveiled on May 7, 1975, on the eve of the 30th anniversary of the victory over fascism. What were the reasons for the pacification? Based on the preserved information provided from those who survived the pacification, today we can say that the reasons for the pacification were several. The main thing, however, was the desire to intimidate the inhabitants to show that it was not worth helping the guerrillas and fighting for freedom. However, Sulkowice cannot be regarded as an isolated example of such beastlyness. The pacification of villages and towns in 1943 and 1944 in Małopolska was very much, and the German army almost specialized in carrying out such actions. Let those events remain in our memory forever. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Hello and glory to all those who died at the hands of an aggressor during German aggression. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-15-sejm-37-1.u389	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Despite many years of tradition in the field of the care of monuments, there is still a problem in Poland of destruction of objects of historical, artistic and sentimental value. The lack of interest in them causes an important element of our culture to fade into oblivion, and from year to year the chance to save them is decreasing. It's hard to tell what's causing it. The issue of financial resources is not always crucial here. A family example of such an object is a Dutch windmill in Krasocin, which is the only preserved windmill in the Italian district. In Świętokrzyskie voivodship there were two brick windmills: one krasociński, the other located in Szwarszowice, Ostrowiecki district. A brick windmill in Krasocin was built around 1920 in place of its 19th-century predecessor made of wood, which was already a observation point for Austrian and Russian troops during World War I. In January 1945, during the offensive, the object was almost destroyed by the Russians and since then, from year to year, it became more and more ruined. This was the case until 1980, in which thanks to the efforts of the authorities of the municipality of Krasocin the windmill was renovated, and a year later it created the Museum of Bread, which was invented by Felix Rak. 20 years later, as a result of the windstorm, the windmill lost its wings, and the regulation of its legal status made it into private hands. It was only at the end of 2017 that the municipality of Krasocin once again owned it, and thanks to the support of the Minister of Culture and National Heritage under the programme "Protection of Monuments', the windmill was revitalized. Today, as in the 1980s, it is a tourist attraction in which the Krasock Museum of Bread is located. The object in its walls collects many exhibits connected with baking bread. The workshops organized here are an important cultural and educational element for the youngest, they teach respect for bread and the work of farmers, and for whole families they are a multigenerational tour. The windmill is a unique showcase of the municipality of Krasocin and its symbol. The long struggle for his rescue resulted in the expected result - he was saved from oblivion. There are many objects like him in Poland. It's up to us what their fate will be. The ceremonial opening of the Krasock Museum of Bread will take place on 25 September 2021. I cordially invite you to visit the facility, not only on this day. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-15-sejm-37-1.u401	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I regret to inform you that on September 12th this year. Janina Kalinowska, President of the Association of the Remembrance of Poles Murdered in Volyn, left us and survived the Volyn crime, a great warrior for the truth of the Volyn slaughter and the Eastern Kres. She has always fought for the memory of Poles murdered in Volyn and for calling this beastly crime genocide. Janina Kalinowska was very close to us. For years, the Independent Self-Governmental Association of Individual Farmers (Solidarity) has actively supported the activities of the association in commemorating victims of genocide by Ukrainian nationalists on Poles murdered in Volhynia and those who survived this terrible tragedy. Janina Kalinowska was probably born in 1935. The date of her birth was determined by the court, she did not know her. She was raised in the colony of Funduma, a village of Polish military settlers in the county of Włodzimierz where she lived with her family. On 11 July 1943, Ukrainian nationalists, the faction of Stepan Bandera and its armed arm - the Ukrainian Uprising Army, attacked this town, killing in a cruel way almost all the inhabitants of 260 people. They also murdered her parents and her 2-year-old brother. Miss Janina survived miraculously. As she later emphasized: I was lucky because my parents and my brother were not killed by an axe, fork, or other murder weapon, but were shot. I don't remember much of it. When the shots were fired, my mom, falling down, must have dragged me along. Later she was hidden by a neighbor, a friend of her father's, led her to Włodzimierz, where she was taken care of by a Polish family with whom Mrs Janina was sent to work in Germany. From May 1945, she was an orphan after the war in a transitional camp in Germany. In 1948, the family she was staying with brought her to Poland. She went to a child's home in Zamość run by the Polish Social Welfare Committee. Mrs. Janina Kalinowski's life motto was memory and the truth about Volyn. She witnessed the sad and tragic truth about Poles murdered with the greatest cruelty the world has ever known. As part of the Association of Remembrance of Poles Murdered in Volhynia, she organized a number of trips to Volhynia, so that light and folding flowers would be lit with symbolic crosses placed on the places of mass murder. She also took a number of initiatives to commemorate Poles murdered in Volyn. Janina Kalinowska, led by the Association of Remembrance of Poles Murdered in Volyn, was honoured with two extremely important titles: the Custos of National Remembrance in 2013 and the Guardian of Remembrance in 2019. Her testimony was an inspiration to the creation of many books, press articles, videos and films. I believe that her life's way will help future generations to follow in her footsteps and continue the work of memory, as well as look at the Volyn of our ancestors as a beautiful land in the Bug basin, where only crosses, abandoned cemeteries and places remind us of Polish hosts. She herself will always remain in our kind memory as ambassador of the Volyn land and the Eastern Kres. The eternal room of her memory. [[Applause]]

Stanisław Dąbek, an honorary citizen of the city of Lubaczów and also a student of the Lubaczów school in 1905 and 1907, who spent his childhood in the village of Felsendorf, currently Dąbków. Stanisław Dąbek is one of the honoured traditions of the Polish Army. In the evening of 19 September 1939, in the area of Babie Dołów, in the face of an inevitable disaster, he took his life with a shot to the head and ordered him to stop fighting immediately after his death. On 30 August 1957, on the grave of Col. The oak at the Redłów Coast Defender's Cemetery has been unveiled a tombstone with carved words.I will show you how the Pole fights and dies. What caused this, ladies and gentlemen? An example of the district of kolbrzeg, in which some 1400 hosts are managed, about 19 thousand. ha. The farmers counted their crop of basic cereals: rape, wheat, rye, triticale, oats and, ladies and gentlemen, calculated that they collected an average of 2 t less per hectare. They calculated the average for all these cereals and they came out, ladies and gentlemen, for the district itself, a loss of PLN 100 million this year. One county in Zachodniopomorskie Voivodship. But what happened, ladies and gentlemen? The Ministry of Agriculture created a drought application last year. The farmer must submit an application to the restructuring agency. What's going on? He may submit, generate a drought application if the loss on the holding is more than 30%. They've lost a lot, they're asking for a hearing, they're asking once again so that they can get some support, because even if the prices for nitrogen fertilisers (the bell), I don't know if you know they've increased by more than 50% this year. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-15-sejm-37-1.u410	A wound that will never heal. In one of my previous speeches, I mentioned the losses my land suffered in World War II. Consciousness of these losses is not always widely seen today, which is understandable. Memory is unreliable and displaces those events that have been difficult or even traumatic for us. Some such events are sometimes known only by historians, after all they are closest to the past and they also shape it through their narrative work in some way. For several centuries, Piotrków was the seat of the Crown Court, the supreme court of appeal for matters of earthly law. A similar tribunal was in Lublin. This institution founded by wise King Stefan Batore settled for a long time difficult disputes between the nobleman: in Piotrków for Wielkopolska and in Lublin for Małopolska. Ten years of operation of this element of the noble government in the complex system of the judiciary and the Republic of Poland produced a huge amount of archival material. The file contained complete descriptions of cases taking place before the court of Piotrków. Their dim echoes can be found in the excellent book by Valery Łozinski - Right and Left. After the third partition in 1795 the documents were dispersed, but their chopping remained in different places in Piotrków. Most of them were in the city archives in time. After Poland regained its independence in 1918, as part of the centralization of archives, documents of the Crown Court in Piotrków and the Piotrków City Books found safe refuge in Warsaw archives. When another war broke out, they initially did not arouse special interest in the Germans and were relatively peaceful until August 1944. The Warsaw Uprising was tragic not only in its human dimension. Not only the lives of thousands of Polish soldiers and civilians, but also hundreds of thousands of priceless souvenirs of the past were placed on the altar of sacrifice. All the Piotrków documents burned down in Warsaw. Someone might say it's not time to regret the roses when the woods are on fire. But I will ask: Why not? I told you here about the Piotrków documents, that's just an example. During the war started by the Germans and the Soviets, they deliberately destroyed thousands of works of art and Polish historical monuments. Are these wounds deep enough? There is a unique place in the National Library in Warsaw. It is a urn in which there are several handfuls of books and documents burned by the Germans. It's very impressive. Among all countries participating in World War II Poland suffered the greatest losses in historical sources. This is the wound I'd like to remind you of. We've lost a huge part of our heritage. This war trauma will never end, and the wound will never heal.
